## assistant

You’re right. **Conditional does not yet match Intro to LR’s structure closely enough.** I built the curriculum, but used a simpler interface.

| Intro to LR | Conditional now |
|---|---|
| Integrated sequence of notes, tools, checkpoints, drills, practice, and capstones | Lesson list with a shared practice screen |
| Learning-path rail visible while working | General section navigation |
| Previous/next actions and lesson contents | Recommended next step, longer lesson pages |
| Shared workspace with reference/tool drawer | Separate reference pages |
| Distinct guided learning experiences | Repeated lesson and question template |

**The assignment lock is shared by both bootcamps.** It is a separate account-access issue.

My recommendation is to preserve the conditional content and rebuild its presentation around Intro’s workspace and integrated action path: **learn → guided activity → skill drill → review → mixed application**, with your three difficulty levels and targeted assignments retained.

I overstated how closely the first release matched Intro. It needs this structural pass.

## My request:
shouldnta havesequence o diffuculty it should allow them tthe stage again the curriculum according to where hte yare atw thehter e hteyaero conditional


## assistant

Yes. **The curriculum should start students where they are, rather than make everyone complete Beginner, then Medium, then Advanced.**

There should be two ways through it:

- **From the beginning:** a guided teaching sequence for someone new to conditionals.
- **From their current stage:** assign the exact skills and difficulty they need, skipping what they already know.

A student could need Beginner Group 3 language, Medium linking, and Advanced contraposition at the same time. Their curriculum should accommodate that uneven skill profile.

Difficulty should be selectable within each skill. The teaching sequence provides direction; it should not force every student through the same difficulty progression.

## assistant

Understood. **You assign their starting point and activities. Students follow those assignments; they do not select a difficulty level.**

Levels 1–3 describe the demands of individual activities at different points in the curriculum. They are not three complete passes through the course.

You can assign the full curriculum from the beginning, start someone at a later stage, or assign one specific activity to address a gap. The curriculum’s order follows how conditional skills build on one another.

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

Necessary vs. Sufficient Presenting a conditional rule and then misusing it by either reversing or negating it improperly (illegal reversal or illegal negation). • *Example:* People who have seen *The 13th* have a great understanding of the US prison system. Since Megan hasn’t seen it, she must have a poor understanding of the US prison system. • *Example:* People who have seen *The 13th* have a great understanding of the US prison system. Jane understands the system well, so clearly sh\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional.gdoc\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Conditional.gdoc  **Modified:** 2025-10-17T08:42:29.714000+00:00  **Words:** 658  **Doc ID:** 10e6Okb__aaEYdU2T2gmAD95tnbTy4KYtrggk3Stl4sQ\\n\\nConditional logic forms the bedrock of many arguments and inferences tested on the LSAT requiring test-takers to demonstrate proficiency in recognizing the contrapositive logic and avoiding common illegal moves. While the provided sources do not detail every single LSAT Logical Reasoning question type (such as Parallel Reasoning or Method of Reasoning) they offer a comprehensive breakdown of the core activities required for analysis and specifically target the most critical error tested in Flaw questions. The breakdown of conditional logic primarily revolves around two main analytical activities: Chaining (deriving new rules) and Triggering (deriving new facts). I. General Breakdown Activity: Chaining Conditionals (Deriving New Rules). Chaining involves synthesizing multiple conditional rules to create a new inferred conditional statement. This is crucial for questions that require you to draw broad conclusions from a set of premises (often seen in Inference/Must Be True contexts). Connecting Rules: You must determine if the necessary condition (right side) of one conditional statement matches the sufficient condition (left side) of another. For instance if A rightarrow B and B rightarrow C they chain to infer a new conditional relationship: A rightarrow C. Contraposing Chains: You can contraposit an entire chain starting from the final necessary condition and negating back to the initial sufficient condition. If the chain is A rightarrow D the contrapositive is If not D then not A. The LSAT commonly rewards the ability to connect the beginning of the chain to the ending of the chain. Negating Complex Ideas: If the idea being contraposed (the trigger or the outcome) is a complex idea connected by and or or (e.g. A and B) you must remember to flip the truth values when negating. And turns into or and or turns into and. This ensures you correctly flip the truth value of everything in the complex idea. II. General Breakdown Activity: Triggering Conditionals (Deriving New Facts). Triggering is the process of using a known fact to activate a conditional rule and derive a certain factual inference. This is the process utilized in many Must Be True and Inference questions. A conditional rule (Sufficient rightarrow Necessary) can only be used in two valid scenarios: 1. Affirming the Sufficient Condition: If a fact confirms that the sufficient condition (the left side the trigger) is true then the necessary condition (the right side the outcome) must necessarily follow. 2. Denying the Necessary Condition (Contrapositive Logic): If a fact confirms that the necessary condition (the right side the outcome) is not true then the sufficient condition (the left side the trigger) cannot have been true. If the fact relates to the conditional rule in any other way (e.g. affirming the necessary or denying the sufficient) the rule just disappears and has no power over us. III. Breakdown in Specific Question Types and Contexts. Flaw Questions. Flaw questions are specifically designed to test whether you can recognize errors in conditional reasoning. The Conditional Flaw: The specific mistake frequently made by LSAT authors is confusing the necessary and the sufficient. This happens when an argument presents a rule (A rightarrow B) observes that the right side (the necessary condition B) is happening and then attempts to conclude that this proves the left side (the sufficient condition A) is happening. Breakdown Method: Your breakdown must identify the conditional rule and then recognize that the author has committed the illegal move of reading the conditional backward. This is the primary error the LSAT tries to ensure students avoid. For example learning that Alice is in the USA (necessary condition) does not allow you to conclude she is in NYC (sufficient condition). Inference and Must Be True Questions. These questions rely heavily on the ability to correctly apply the chaining and triggering activities described above. Breakdown Method: When presented with premises (rules) and facts you must diagram the conditionals and perform valid inferences. You must look for ways to chain rules together to find a connection from the premises to the answer choices.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional Understanding.gdoc\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Conditional Understanding.gdoc  **Modified:** 2025-08-13T02:18:08.873000+00:00  **Words:** 603  **Doc ID:** 141zf_n8I7K0nB5Gmohu3hqAxJWgKVP2GRT6Xo7UpFrc\\n\\nFiguring Out The Language. Lets tackle the biggest challenge in conditional reasoning figuring out the language. Well start by returning to our first example and pointing out the target of each indicator word. The indicators target is the word or phrase it is referring to. If Im breathing then air is present. Each instance of breathing has air. When Im breathing air is present. Im breathing only if air is present. Any breathing means air is present. Only the presence of air allows for breathing. All instances of breathing have air present. Breathing depends on air being present. Every instance of breathing has air present. I need air to breathe. In order to breathe I must have air. I have to have air if Im breathing. People who breathe need air. Notice that in each of these examples the indicator is right before its target. This will be the case in the majority of conditional statements you read but acting on the generalization that the indicator is always immediately before its target will lead you into trouble. Air must be present for breathing. Air is essential for breathing. Air is necessary for breathing. Air is a precondition for breathing. Air is required for breathing. In these examples the target precedes the indicator. Why do they have to make it so difficult you ask? Because the LSAT wants you to understand what the sentence is really saying. They dont want you to see an indicator word and go on autopilot. You also cannot generalize that these specific necessary indicators will always come after their target. Each of them can switch sides. THE WHAT TEST. The way you can get around all this ambiguity is by taking the time to actually understand what the sentence is telling you. When you see an indicator and you have even a little doubt about its target ask yourself one question: What is the indicator referring to? The What Test is simple. You can perform it by adding a what to whichever side of the indicator makes grammatical sense. Heres how you perform the What Test: If what? When what? Any what? All what? Every what? In order to what? People who what? Each what? Must what? Whats necessary? Whats required? Only if what? Depends on what? Need what? Have to what? Whats essential? Whats the precondition? The What Test will probably not be necessary for the indicators like if and all because their target is clear. With some of the more confusing necessary indicators like depends and required you may use the What Test more. Air is required for breathing. I require air to breathe. The What Test probably seems simple to you and it is. However it is only simple because Im taking the split second to actually understand the sentence and figure out what require is referring to. You can do this too just be sure to take your time with conditionals in the beginning and youll be lightning quick in a couple weeks. Your Friend the Personalized If/Then. Personalized if/thens are translations for conditionals. Heres another safeguard on conditional diagramming. Make a habit of translating conditionals into personalized if/thens. Personalized if/thens require you to put yourself in the conditional statement and ask yourself what you know about your situation. This can get you out of confusion panic and back to the real world. HOW TO CREATE A PERSONALIZED IF/THEN. Imagine youre confused on what a conditional means. Instead of panicking: 1. Try to understand the casual non-conditional meaning of the sentence. 2. Take a guess at which half of the statement is the sufficient condition.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional logic intro_.pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/Conditional logic intro_.pdf  **Modified:** 2024-09-23T20:04:05+00:00  **Words:** 580  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nUnderstanding Conditional Logic and Contrapositives in LSAT Logical Reasoning Clarifying Conditional Logic Conditional reasoning, or \\"the art of the if/then,\\" is a fundamental concept on the LSAT that helps you understand logical relationships within arguments. Sufficient and Necessary Conditions: A Practical Example Imagine this scenario: Conditional Statement: \\"If you shop at Macy\'s, then you will get a special discount.\\" What do you need to do to get that \\"special discount\\"? If you think \\"sho\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional Worksheet Intro.docx\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Conditional Worksheet Intro.docx  **Modified:** 2025-07-22T01:03:44.050000+00:00  **Words:** 547  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nConditional Reasoning Worksheet Name: _________________________ Date: _________________________ Component 1: Indicator Identification & Group Label (13 items) Instructions: Circle the underlined word in each sentence, then—without notes—write “1” if it signals a sufficient condition or “2” if it signals a necessary condition. Focus on finding Conditional Indicator words and using them as a way to organize how you write or think about the If-Then rule. Write or think through the contrapositive as well. Beware illegal Reversals or Negations. Conditional Logic by type Recap: Principle (the left side of the principle matches the evidence, the right side matches the conclusion). Parallel (diagramming these arguments with algebraic recipes allows us to be more picky and decisive when evaluating answers). Assumption (the conclusion gives us an A to Z logic path we want to prove, and the evidence + the correct answer choice assemble together to create that A to Z path). Must Be True / Most Supported (multiple conditionals often chain together, and provided facts often match the contrapositive-trigger of a conditional and allow us to infer the contrapositive-outcome). Flaw / Parallel Flaw (flaw + conditional logic usually means the necessary vs sufficient flaw using a rule in an illegal backwards or opposite fashion). instructions: Try these 10 questions in 22.5 minutes. Try to answer them all within your time limit, then circle back and confirm your thinking (untimed) before confirming your answers and reading the explanations, if available. CONDITIONAL KEYWORDS: Put on Left (sufficient conditions): if, when, any, each, all, every, the only, whenever, whoever. Put on Right (necessary conditions): only if, must, requires, ensures, always, implies, presupposes, depends on, guarantees. If-Not (negated version on left): unless, without, until, except. Negative to Positive conversions: No A are B = All A are not B. Not all A are B = Some A are not B. Few A are B = Most A are not B. Problems to Complete (10 total): PrepTest 130 Section 3 Question 13. PrepTest 130 Section 3 Question 14. PrepTest 131 Section 1 Question 16. PrepTest 131 Section 1 Question 19. PrepTest 134 Section 2 Question 3. PrepTest 134 Section 2 Question 22. PrepTest 134 Section 2 Question 23. PrepTest 135 Section 2 Question 15. PrepTest 125 Section 4 Question 23. PrepTest 125 Section 4 Question 25. Pause! For every mistake you made in this set, complete the sections below in as much detail as possible: Why did I eliminate the right answer? Why did I choose this wrong answer? What can I do next time to get this right? Additional notes.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: intuition technique when stuck( conditional logic) .pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/intuition technique when stuck( conditional logic) .pdf  **Modified:** 2024-05-16T01:17:45+00:00  **Words:** 370  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nHow to Figure Out Which Is Which: The What Test vs. Personalized If/Then The What Test The What Test is designed to resolve ambiguities in sentences with conditional indicators by pinpointing exactly what the indicator refers to. How to Perform the What Test This test involves adding a \\"what\\" to whichever side of the indicator makes grammatical sense, helping clarify the target of the condition. If what? People who what? Depends on what? When what? Each what? Need what? Any what? Must what? Have\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: intuition technique when stuck( conditional logic) .gdoc\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/intuition technique when stuck( conditional logic) .gdoc  **Modified:** 2025-06-23T03:31:37.970000+00:00  **Words:** 370  **Doc ID:** 1ZthkbR3x5oHk9Rqv2ZOzR6j_tZ4HDsM45S_K0hBZlpA\\n\\nHow to Figure Out Which Is Which The What Test vs. Personalized If/Then. The What Test. The What Test is designed to resolve ambiguities in sentences with conditional indicators by pinpointing exactly what the indicator refers to. How to Perform the What Test. This test involves adding a what to whichever side of the indicator makes grammatical sense helping clarify the target of the condition. If what? People who what? Depends on what? When what? Each what? Need what? Any what? Must what? Have to what? All what? Whats necessary? Whats essential? Every what? Whats required? Whats the precondition? In order to what? Only (if) what? This test is typically unnecessary for straightforward indicators like if and all but proves invaluable for more confusing indicators such as depends and required. Example of The What Test. Sentence: Air is required for breathing. Implementation: Question: What is required in this sentence? Answer: Air is the thing that is required. Personalized If/Then Strategy. Personalized If/Then is a technique used to translate conditional statements into personal contexts simplifying understanding and ensuring correct logical diagramming. How to Create a Personalized If/Then. 1. Understand the casual non-conditional meaning of the sentence. 2. Take a guess at which half of the statement is the sufficient condition. 3. Put yourself in the situation of your guess and ask what you know about yourself. 4. If your guess is correct you will find that the necessary condition has to pertain. Example Translation into Personalized If/Then. Original Statement: Orchids depend on frequent conversation. Casual Meaning: Orchids need frequent conversation. Guess: Orchids as the sufficient condition. Personalization: If Im an orchid what do I know about me? I depend on frequent conversation. If Im an orchid then I have frequent conversation. This method not only aids in understanding the conditional but also verifies its correctness. Incorrect guesses can be easily identified and corrected through this personal context. Advantages of Personalized If/Then. Helps intuitively understand conditionals by imagining yourself as part of the scenario. Provides confidence throughout tests especially in correctly interpreting conditionals not phrased as if/then statements. Both the What Test and Personalized If/Then Strategy are crucial tools in deciphering and applying conditional logic particularly in competitive exams like the LSAT.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional Chains.pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/Conditional Chains.pdf  **Modified:** 2024-07-02T20:46:11+00:00  **Words:** 352  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nConditional Chains Conditional statements are like Legos. Statement 2: If I end up in the highly-caffeinated poor house, I will finally be cool enough to wear non-ironic plaid. Let’s think each of these statements through. Let’s place ourselves in this situation and see what we know. Example Statement: You cant master the LSAT without mastering conditional reasoning. Credited Answers: If you master the LSAT then you master conditional reasoning. If you dont master conditional reasoning you wont master the LSAT. Explanation: If you cant master X without Y Y is required in order to master X. That can be rephrased into if/then format in two ways: 1. If you master X then you have Y. 2. If you dont have Y you cant master X. Tasks: 1. Consider the conditional relationship at play in each statement. 2. Rephrase the statement using if/then format. Statements and Answers. 1. Sentence A policy change will occur only if the board approves it. Translation: Contrapositive: 2. Sentence Keeping plants healthy requires regular watering and sunlight. 3. If–Then Translation Contrapositive 2) The archive requires a passcode. If–Then Translation Contrapositive Part I — Mixed Conditionals 3) Only volunteers sit in the front row. If–Then Translation Contrapositive 4) The attendants tag every folded napkin as reserved. If–Then Translation Contrapositive Part I — Mixed Conditionals 5) No library\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional_Logic_Workbook_MASTER_Combined_v23_FIXEDTEXT_TOPRIGHT.pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Conditional_Logic_Workbook_MASTER_Combined_v23_FIXEDTEXT_TOPRIGHT.pdf  **Modified:** 2025-10-18T23:31:21.340000+00:00  **Words:** 86  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nConditional Chains A Workbook #1 Only volunteers sit in the front row. Diagram / Chain Inference (first → last) Contrapositive (negate & reverse) #2 The attendants tag every folded napkin as reserved. Diagram / Chain Inference (first → last) Contrapositive (negate & reverse) #3 No library card is valid after its expiration date. Diagram / Chain Inference (first → last) Contrapositive (negate & reverse) #4 Entry is permitted to any guest with a blue wristband. Diagram / Chain Inference (first → l\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: 6 step processs.pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/6 step processs.pdf  **Modified:** 2024-10-21T17:24:57+00:00  **Words:** 42  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nSteps to Analyze Conditional Statements: 1. Identify the logical operator. 2. Identify the two main concepts (groups, categories, events, ideas). 3. Assign symbols to the two main concepts. 4. Apply the translation rule. 5. Find the contrapositive. 6. Translate back to English.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: Conditional Logic LSAT question intro.pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/Conditional Logic LSAT question intro.pdf  **Modified:** 2024-07-17T18:42:13+00:00  **Words:** 33  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nConditional Logic Set PrepTest 48, Section 4, Question 16 PrepTest 19, Section 4, Question 4 PrepTest 20, Section 4, Question 12 PrepTest 20, Section 4, Question 24 PrepTest 22, Section 2, Question 5\\n\\n---\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

Identify the two main concepts (groups, c\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: all- most:some( brief) .pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/all- most:some( brief) .pdf  **Modified:** 2024-10-28T21:44:35+00:00  **Words:** 411  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\n• • • • • • • • • • • • “All” Statements X → Y Quantifiers that express totality are translated as conditional statements: All Xs are Ys Any X is also Y Every X is a Y Each X is also a Y No Xs are not Ys None of the Xs are not also Ys “Most” Statements X -most→ Y Quantifiers that express majority are translated like conditional statements, but with an “m” or “most” arrow: Most Xs are Ys The majority of Xs are Ys Xs are usually Y X is typically Y Xs tend to be Ys Few of the Xs fail to be Ys “Some\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: zayd homwork summary.pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/zayd homwork summary.pdf  **Modified:** 2024-09-22T02:09:12+00:00  **Words:** 141  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nSATURDAY, SEP 21, 2024, 10:05 PM 1. • 2. • • • 1. • 2. • 1. • Sunday: Morning: Recall and Rephrase (R&R) Drill: Start the day with the Recall and Rephrase Drill (as outlined in the PDF). Afternoon: Complete Worksheets and Memorization: Work on the Conditional Rephrasing and Contrapositive Worksheet and the Conditional Diagramming Worksheet (as provided in the PDFs). Monday (All Day): Complete 25 LSAT Conditional Lo\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Source: MORE LAWGIC (3&4).pdf\\n\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/MORE LAWGIC (3&4).pdf  **Modified:** 2024-05-21T04:47:38+00:00  **Words:** 140  **Doc ID:** None\\n\\nLSAT Conditional Logic Worksheet Instructions For each statement below, convert it into its conditional form and then provide its contrapositive. Pedagogical provenance: ai-os/wiki/teaching-voice.md,\\n * ai-os/wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md, and ai-os/brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/.\\n * Group taxonomy is taken from src/features/conditional-refresher/content.ts.\\n * All scenarios, questions and explanations below are newly authored, not official LSAT items.\\n * Difficulty concerns interpretation and inference. These sets prescribe no mastery threshold or clock.\\n */\\nconst question = (\\n  id: string, prompt: string, options: string[], answerIndex: number,\\n  hint: string, reasoningSteps: string[],\\n): ConditionalQuestion => ({ id, prompt, options, answerIndex, hint, reasoningSteps, explanation: reasoningSteps.join(\' \') });\\n\\nexport const foundationUnits: ConditionalUnit[] = [\\n  {\\n    id: \'orientation\', title: \'Find the Guarantee\', area: \'understanding\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [],\\n    goal: \'Separate a guarantee, a case fact and a prediction before drawing a conclusion.\',\\n    teaching: [\\n      \'A conditional rule tells you what follows when a condition is met. If a parcel is scanned at intake, its tracking record is updated. Scanning is the starting condition. Updating the record is the guaranteed result.\',\\n      \'The rule does not tell you that any parcel was scanned. A case fact supplies that information: Parcel 18 was scanned at intake. Together, the rule and the fact establish that Parcel 18\\\\\'s tracking record was updated.\',\\n      \'Probably and usually express weaker claims. They do not give an exceptionless guarantee. Keep the whole claim, including its limits. An unknown result is not a false result.\',\\n    ],\\n    method: [\'Underline what the statement promises.\', \'Separate any rule from a fact about one case.\', \'Match the fact to the complete starting condition.\', \'State only the promised result about that case.\'],\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every calibrated gauge receives a blue seal.\', \'Gauge 6 is calibrated.\'], reasoning: [\'The rule applies to calibrated gauges.\', \'Gauge 6 meets that condition.\'], conclusion: \'Gauge 6 receives a blue seal.\' },\\n    guidedExample: question(\'orientation-guide\', \'If a card is activated, its number is registered. Card L is activated. What follows?\', [\'Card L\\\\\'s number is registered.\', \'Every registered number belongs to an activated card.\', \'No other card is registered.\'], 0, \'Match Card L to the condition after if.\', [\'Card L satisfies the activation condition.\', \'The rule guarantees registration for that card.\']),\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Treating a rule as evidence that its starting condition occurred.\', repair: \'Read the rule as a promise about qualifying cases. Look separately for a fact identifying one.\' },\\n    drills: {\\n      beginner: [\\n        question(\'orientation-b1\', \'Which statement gives an exceptionless rule?\', [\'The desk is staffed now.\', \'If the desk is staffed, the bell is answered.\', \'The bell is usually answered.\'], 1, \'Look for a guaranteed connection.\', [\'The if statement links staffing to answering.\', \'The other statements report a case or a tendency.\']),\\n        question(\'orientation-b2\', \'Every numbered crate has a label. Which additional fact lets you conclude that Crate M has a label?\', [\'Crate M is numbered.\', \'Crate M is heavy.\', \'Crate M might be numbered.\'], 0, \'Find a fact that meets the entire starting condition.\', [\'Being numbered is enough for a label.\', \'The first fact establishes that condition for Crate M.\']),\\n        question(\'orientation-b3\', \'Which claim reports a case fact?\', [\'All restored books have new bindings.\', \'Book 4 has a new binding.\', \'Restored books generally look newer.\'], 1, \'A case fact identifies a particular subject.\', [\'Book 4 is one specified book.\', \'The statement reports its binding rather than a general relationship.\']),\\n      ],\\n      medium: [\\n        question(\'orientation-m1\', \'Most inspected lifts operate quietly. Lift 2 was inspected. Is quiet operation guaranteed?\', [\'Yes.\', \'No; quiet operation is impossible.\', \'No; it remains unsettled.\'], 2, \'Most allows exceptions.\', [\'The claim permits some inspected lifts to operate noisily.\', \'Nothing identifies which kind Lift 2 is.\']),\\n        question(\'orientation-m2\', \'If a roof has a solar panel, it has a support frame. A roof has a support frame. What is established?\', [\'It has a solar panel.\', \'It has no solar panel.\', \'Its panel status is unknown.\'], 2, \'The promised result does not establish its source.\', [\'The rule promises a frame for panel-equipped roofs.\', \'It does not exclude frames on other roofs.\']),\\n        question(\'orientation-m3\', \'Every sealed sample is logged. No sample is said to be sealed. Which conclusion follows?\', [\'A logged sample exists.\', \'Every sealed sample is logged, but existence remains unknown.\', \'No samples are logged.\'], 1, \'Distinguish a relationship from an existing member.\', [\'The rule governs any sealed sample.\', \'It does not assert that a sample of that kind exists.\']),\\n      ],\\n      advanced: [\\n        question(\'orientation-a1\', \'Every visitor authorized to enter the archive may request a copy. Imani is a visitor authorized to enter the archive. What is guaranteed?\', [\'Imani requests a copy.\', \'Imani may request a copy.\', \'Imani receives a copy.\'], 1, \'Permission is not an action or a completed outcome.\', [\'The rule grants permission to authorized visitors.\', \'It says neither that Imani uses it nor that a request succeeds.\']),\\n        question(\'orientation-a2\', \'Every sensor certified for underwater use has a sealed housing. Sensor K is certified for indoor use. What follows about its housing?\', [\'It is sealed.\', \'It is not sealed.\', \'Neither conclusion is established.\'], 2, \'Compare the two certification scopes.\', [\'Indoor certification does not establish underwater certification.\', \'The supplied rule therefore gives no housing result for K.\']),\\n        question(\'orientation-a3\', \'The manual says: If the warning light flashes, shut down the machine. The light flashes. What follows from this instruction alone?\', [\'The machine has shut down.\', \'The instruction calls for shutdown.\', \'The machine cannot shut down.\'], 1, \'Keep an instruction separate from compliance.\', [\'The flashing light satisfies the instruction\\\\\'s condition.\', \'That establishes the required response, not whether anyone performed it.\']),\\n      ],\\n    },\\n  },\\n  {\\n    id: \'rules-facts\', title: \'Facts, Rules, and What Follows\', area: \'understanding\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'orientation\'],\\n    goal: \'Apply rules to case facts and distinguish case conclusions from new rules.\',\\n    teaching: [\\n      \'A fact tells you something about a case. A rule connects conditions. Every digitized map is searchable is a rule. Map R is digitized is a fact. Match the fact to the rule\\\\\'s starting condition to conclude that Map R is searchable.\',\\n      \'For digitized → searchable, digitized establishes searchable. Not searchable rules out digitized: a digitized map would have to be searchable. Searchable alone leaves digitization unknown. Not digitized alone leaves searchability unknown.\',\\n      \'Two rules can create a new rule. Digitized → searchable and searchable → indexed give digitized → indexed. They do not establish that an indexed map exists. Add a digitized case as a separate fact to establish an indexed case.\',\\n    ],\\n    method: [\'List rules separately from case facts.\', \'Keep the same subject and exact predicates when matching.\', \'Use a present trigger or an absent required result.\', \'Label the output as a case conclusion or a conditional rule.\'],\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every waterproof notebook has coated pages.\', \'Notebook A does not have coated pages.\'], reasoning: [\'Waterproof status would require coated pages.\', \'That required result is absent for A.\'], conclusion: \'Notebook A is not waterproof.\' },\\n    guidedExample: question(\'rules-facts-guide\', \'Every translated notice is archived. Notice H is archived. What follows about translation?\', [\'H is translated.\', \'H is not translated.\', \'Translation is unknown.\'], 2, \'Archiving is the required result, not the trigger.\', [\'Translated notices must be archived.\', \'Other notices may also be archived, so the fact settles neither translation nor its absence.\']),\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Calling an allowed case a proven case.\', repair: \'Ask whether every situation fitting the information includes the conclusion. If both outcomes fit, say unknown.\' },\\n    drills: {\\n      beginner: [\\n        question(\'rules-facts-b1\', \'Every tested battery has a sticker. Battery P was tested. What follows?\', [\'P has a sticker.\', \'P is the only tested battery.\', \'All stickered batteries were tested.\'], 0, \'Apply the rule to P without adding a reverse rule.\', [\'P meets the tested condition.\', \'The sticker follows for P alone.\']),\\n        question(\'rules-facts-b2\', \'Every polished lens is clear. Lens J is not clear. What follows?\', [\'J is polished.\', \'J is not polished.\', \'J is clear after all.\'], 1, \'Could a polished lens lack the required clarity?\', [\'Polishing would require clarity.\', \'J lacks clarity, so J cannot be polished under the rule.\']),\\n        question(\'rules-facts-b3\', \'Every reserved seat has a marker. Seat W is not reserved. Its marker status is:\', [\'Guaranteed present.\', \'Guaranteed absent.\', \'Unknown.\'], 2, \'The rule speaks only about reserved seats.\', [\'An unreserved seat does not activate the rule.\', \'A marker may be present or absent without breaking it.\']),\\n      ],\\n      medium: [\\n        question(\'rules-facts-m1\', \'Every encrypted file is restricted. Every restricted file is logged. No case fact is supplied. Which follows?\', [\'Some logged files exist.\', \'Every encrypted file is logged.\', \'Every logged file is encrypted.\'], 1, \'Match the shared restricted condition.\', [\'Encryption leads to restriction, which leads to logging.\', \'The resulting rule does not assert a file exists.\']),\\n        question(\'rules-facts-m2\', \'Every approved replacement part is stocked. Part D is a replacement part, but approval is unknown. What follows?\', [\'D is stocked.\', \'D is not stocked.\', \'Stocking remains unknown.\'], 2, \'Replacement part and approved replacement part differ.\', [\'The trigger includes approval.\', \'The supplied fact does not meet that entire trigger.\']),\\n        question(\'rules-facts-m3\', \'Every public hearing is recorded. Hearing N is recorded. A student concludes it is public. Which repair is accurate?\', [\'Recorded → public was never given.\', \'Public hearings cannot be recorded.\', \'The rule has no starting condition.\'], 0, \'Identify the direction the student added.\', [\'The rule runs from public status to recording.\', \'The student used its unsupported reverse.\']),\\n      ],\\n      advanced: [\\n        question(\'rules-facts-a1\', \'Every eligible applicant may appeal. Tavi is an eligible applicant. Which follows?\', [\'Tavi has appealed.\', \'Tavi may appeal.\', \'Tavi\\\\\'s appeal was accepted.\'], 1, \'Preserve the modal word may.\', [\'Tavi satisfies the complete eligible-applicant condition, which grants appeal permission.\', \'Permission establishes neither action nor acceptance.\']),\\n        question(\'rules-facts-a2\', \'Every licensed guide must carry a radio. Guide Z carries no radio. What follows about compliance?\', [\'Z cannot be licensed in real life.\', \'If Z is licensed, Z violates the stated requirement.\', \'Z must be unlicensed in real life.\'], 1, \'A requirement can be violated; it is not a physical impossibility.\', [\'A licensed Z would be required to carry a radio.\', \'The fact would show noncompliance, rather than proving absence of a license.\']),\\n        question(\'rules-facts-a3\', \'Every shortlisted design is displayed. Every displayed design is catalogued. Design E is not catalogued. What follows?\', [\'E is shortlisted.\', \'E is not shortlisted.\', \'No design is displayed.\'], 1, \'Trace what shortlisting would force for E.\', [\'Shortlisting would lead through display to cataloguing.\', \'E lacks the final required result, so E is not shortlisted.\']),\\n      ],\\n    },\\n  },\\n  {\\n    id: \'sufficient-necessary\', title: \'Enough to Guarantee, Required to Follow\', area: \'understanding\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'rules-facts\'],\\n    goal: \'Identify sufficient and necessary roles and test the four case combinations.\',\\n    teaching: [\\n      \'If a window is impact-rated, it has reinforced glass. Impact-rated is sufficient for reinforced glass: it is enough to guarantee it. Reinforced glass is necessary for impact-rated status: the rating cannot occur without it under this descriptive rule.\',\\n      \'Sufficient and necessary name roles in a particular relationship. Reinforced glass may be the sufficient condition of a different rule. It is not permanently a necessary condition.\',\\n      \'The single forbidden combination is impact-rated with no reinforced glass. The other three combinations fit the rule. Reinforced glass alone does not prove the rating. No rating does not prove no reinforced glass.\',\\n    ],\\n    method: [\'Read the rule as if this starting condition holds, that result must hold.\', \'Name the sufficient condition and necessary condition completely.\', \'Look for a trigger paired with an absent required result.\', \'Distinguish consistency with the rule from a fact established by the rule.\'],\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every certified helmet has an inner liner.\', \'A helmet has an inner liner but is not certified.\'], reasoning: [\'Certification requires a liner.\', \'The rule does not require every lined helmet to be certified.\'], conclusion: \'This combination fits the rule; it does not reverse it.\' },\\n    guidedExample: question(\'sufficient-necessary-guide\', \'Every verified account has a recovery code. Which combination violates the rule?\', [\'Verified, with a recovery code.\', \'Verified, without a recovery code.\', \'Unverified, with a recovery code.\'], 1, \'Look for the trigger without its required result.\', [\'Verification is sufficient for a recovery code.\', \'A verified account without one breaks that guarantee.\']),\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Treating a necessary condition as sufficient.\', repair: \'A required ingredient need not be a complete recipe. Ask whether the reverse relationship was actually supplied.\' },\\n    drills: {\\n      beginner: [\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-b1\', \'If a cabinet is fire-rated, it has a steel shell. Which condition does this rule identify as guaranteeing a steel shell?\', [\'Being fire-rated.\', \'Having a steel shell.\', \'Not being fire-rated.\'], 0, \'Find the condition the if clause presents as its trigger.\', [\'Fire-rated status is the condition the rule presents as sufficient.\', \'It guarantees the steel-shell result; the result itself is not the starting condition identified by this rule.\']),\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-b2\', \'Every accredited course has an assessment. What is necessary for accreditation?\', [\'Absence of an assessment.\', \'Having an assessment.\', \'Being an evening course.\'], 1, \'What must an accredited course have?\', [\'Accreditation cannot occur without an assessment under the rule.\', \'An assessment is therefore necessary, though not stated sufficient.\']),\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-b3\', \'Every packaged tube has a cap. Which case fits the rule?\', [\'Packaged, no cap.\', \'Not packaged, with a cap.\', \'Packaged, no cap, but small.\'], 1, \'The rule restricts packaged tubes only.\', [\'A capped unpackaged tube does not break the guarantee.\', \'Both packaged uncapped cases do.\']),\\n      ],\\n      medium: [\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-m1\', \'For polished → reflective and reflective → visible, reflective is:\', [\'Necessary in both rules.\', \'Necessary in the first; sufficient in the second.\', \'Sufficient in both rules.\'], 1, \'Name its role separately on each arrow.\', [\'Reflective is the required result of polishing.\', \'It is the starting condition that guarantees visibility.\']),\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-m2\', \'…12376 tokens truncated…    ],\\n    },\\n  },\\n  {\\n    id: \'implicit-conditionals\', title: \'Recognize a Guarantee without a Familiar Indicator\', area: \'translation\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'mixed-translation\'],\\n    goal: \'Translate guarantees expressed through verbs, definitions and restricted subjects.\',\\n    teaching: [\\n      \'The relationship can appear without if or only if. A certified seal guarantees traceability gives certified seal → traceability. Traceability is a prerequisite for listing gives listed → traceable. Ask what the sentence makes enough and what it makes required.\',\\n      \'Definitions can supply both directions, but only when the definition is explicit. Here, a dormant account means an account with no transactions for a year identifies the defined category. Do not turn a loose description such as dormant accounts tend to be old into a definition or an exceptionless rule.\',\\n      \'Preserve the distinction between guarantee, permission and evidence. A grant authorizes travel gives a permission; it does not prove the traveler leaves. A wet pavement suggests rain does not guarantee rain. Active and passive phrasing can express the same rule without changing the subject involved.\',\\n    ],\\n    method: [\'Find the guarantee, prerequisite, exclusion or explicit definition.\', \'Write what is enough and what is required in complete predicates.\', \'Retain the force of verbs such as authorizes, suggests and requires.\', \'Test whether a counterexample would violate the actual wording.\'],\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'A checksum match ensures that the stored copy is intact.\'], reasoning: [\'The match supplies a guarantee, rather than a mere clue.\', \'The result concerns the stored copy\\\\\'s integrity.\'], conclusion: \'Checksum matches → stored copy intact.\' },\\n    guidedExample: question(\'implicit-conditionals-guide\', \'Identity verification is a prerequisite for issuing a replacement card. Which follows?\', [\'Verified identity → card issued.\', \'Replacement card issued → identity verified.\', \'No card issued → identity unverified.\'], 1, \'A prerequisite must be met for the restricted outcome.\', [\'Issuance requires verification.\', \'Verification is not stated sufficient for issuance.\']),\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Treating any association as a guarantee.\', repair: \'Compare ensures with suggests. Translate the author\\\\\'s degree of commitment, not a stronger relationship.\' },\\n    drills: {\\n      beginner: [\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-b1\', \'A successful self-test guarantees that the indicator lights. Choose the rule.\', [\'Self-test succeeds → indicator lights.\', \'Indicator lights → self-test succeeds.\', \'Self-test fails → indicator does not light.\'], 0, \'Guarantees identifies the sufficient event.\', [\'Self-test success supplies the guarantee.\', \'The sentence leaves other ways to light the indicator open.\']),\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-b2\', \'A signature is a prerequisite for approval. Which follows?\', [\'Signed → approved.\', \'Approved → signed.\', \'Not approved → not signed.\'], 1, \'A prerequisite belongs on the necessary side.\', [\'Approval requires a signature.\', \'A signature alone is not declared sufficient.\']),\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-b3\', \'A valid access token ensures successful entry. Entry is unsuccessful. What follows?\', [\'The token was not valid.\', \'The token was valid.\', \'The entrant was not a person.\'], 0, \'Apply absence of the guaranteed result.\', [\'A valid token would ensure entry.\', \'Unsuccessful entry excludes token validity under this stipulated rule.\']),\\n      ],\\n      medium: [\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-m1\', \'A sealed housing is ensured by factory certification. Which is the direction?\', [\'Housing sealed → factory certification.\', \'Factory certification → housing sealed.\', \'No certification → housing unsealed.\'], 1, \'By identifies the source of the guarantee in this passive sentence.\', [\'Factory certification is what ensures sealing.\', \'The passive phrasing does not reverse that relationship.\']),\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-m2\', \'Residue on a filter suggests that the intake was blocked. What rule is established?\', [\'Residue → blocked intake.\', \'Blocked intake → residue.\', \'Neither exceptionless rule.\'], 2, \'Suggests is weaker than guarantees.\', [\'The statement treats residue as evidence.\', \'It does not rule out residue without blockage or blockage without residue.\']),\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-m3\', \'A digital pass authorizes its holder to use the express lane. Which follows for a holder?\', [\'The holder uses the lane.\', \'The holder is authorized to use the lane.\', \'Everyone using the lane has a digital pass.\'], 1, \'Keep authorizes in the result.\', [\'Holding the pass guarantees the stated authorization.\', \'It establishes neither exercise of that permission nor an exclusive source of permission.\']),\\n      ],\\n      advanced: [\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-a1\', \'A device\\\\\'s eligibility for the rebate depends on certification for indoor operation. Device T is certified only for outdoor operation. What follows?\', [\'T is eligible.\', \'T is not eligible.\', \'Outdoor certification guarantees indoor certification.\'], 1, \'Only outdoor means the required indoor certification is absent.\', [\'Eligibility requires indoor certification.\', \'The stated restriction to outdoor certification excludes that requirement.\']),\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-a2\', \'In this catalogue, a pending record is defined as a record awaiting review. Record B awaits review. What follows?\', [\'B is a pending record under this definition.\', \'B has been reviewed.\', \'Every catalogue uses that definition.\'], 0, \'An explicit definition applies within its stated setting.\', [\'The catalogue definition identifies awaiting-review records as pending.\', \'It applies to B without extending the definition to other catalogues.\']),\\n        question(\'implicit-conditionals-a3\', \'A transfer\\\\\'s completion is contingent on the receiving bank accepting it. The receiving bank accepted Transfer L. Which follows?\', [\'L completed.\', \'L did not complete.\', \'Completion remains unknown.\'], 2, \'Contingent on states a requirement rather than a sufficient cause.\', [\'Completion requires acceptance.\', \'Acceptance alone does not rule out other barriers to completion.\']),\\n      ],\\n    },\\n  },\\n  {\\n    id: \'direct-linking\', title: \'Connect Rules through the Same Condition\', area: \'linking\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'implicit-conditionals\'],\\n    goal: \'Build a directed chain and distinguish its new conditional from a case conclusion.\',\\n    teaching: [\\n      \'Every calibrated meter is registered. Every registered meter is listed. A calibrated meter must therefore be listed: calibrated → registered → listed. The middle condition matches exactly and the arrows continue in the same direction.\',\\n      \'That chain creates a rule, not an existing calibrated meter. Meter D is calibrated adds the fact needed to conclude that D is listed. A listed meter alone does not prove calibration.\',\\n      \'Match complete meanings, not shared words. Registered for field use differs from registered for laboratory use. Keep the same subject through the chain. Rules concerning an object\\\\\'s owner or maker do not automatically transfer that property back to the object.\',\\n      \'A link can be hidden until you take a contrapositive. Every tagged specimen is retained. Every discarded specimen is not retained. The second rule is equivalent to retained → not discarded. Now tagged → retained → not discarded is a forward chain. Write the equivalent rule first; do not simply reverse an arrow. If both sides already contain negatives, cancel the double negatives when contraposing.\',\\n    ],\\n    method: [\'Translate each rule into complete conditions.\', \'Find a result-to-trigger match; if needed, form an equivalent contrapositive to expose it.\', \'Follow only forward arrows in the rules or their valid contrapositives to create a new rule.\', \'Apply a separately supplied case fact, or preserve the result as a rule if no fact is given.\'],\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every audited report is certified.\', \'Every certified report is published.\', \'Report R is audited.\'], reasoning: [\'Audited → certified → published.\', \'R satisfies the first condition, so both required results follow for R.\'], conclusion: \'Report R is certified and published.\' },\\n    guidedExample: question(\'direct-linking-guide\', \'Every keyed cabinet is numbered. Every numbered cabinet is catalogued. Which follows from these rules alone?\', [\'Some catalogued cabinet exists.\', \'Every keyed cabinet is catalogued.\', \'Every catalogued cabinet is keyed.\'], 1, \'Link the shared numbered condition without adding a case.\', [\'Keyed leads to numbered and then catalogued.\', \'The result is a new conditional, without existence or a reverse direction.\']),\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Joining rules merely because they use similar nouns.\', repair: \'Compare the full middle predicates and subject identities. A link needs the same condition, not a related topic.\' },\\n    drills: {\\n      beginner: [\\n        question(\'direct-linking-b1\', \'Every glazed tile is polished. Every polished tile is inspected. What follows?\', [\'Glazed tile → inspected tile.\', \'Inspected tile → glazed tile.\', \'Unglazed tile → uninspected tile.\'], 0, \'Follow the polished middle condition.\', [\'Glazing guarantees polishing.\', \'Polishing guarantees inspection, so glazing guarantees inspection.\']),\\n        question(\'direct-linking-b2\', \'Every sealed bottle is labelled. What follows?\', [\'A is boxed.\', \'Every boxed bottle is sealed.\', \'A is the only boxed bottle.\'], 0, \'Move A forward through both rules.\', [\'A\\\\\'s sealing gives labelling.\', \'That labelling gives boxing, without identifying other bottles.\']),\\n        question(\'direct-linking-b3\', \'Every enrolled intern is assigned a desk. Which is the middle condition?\', [\'Having an entry code.\', \'Being assigned a desk.\', \'Being an intern regardless of enrollment.\'], 1, \'Find the first rule\\\\\'s result repeated as the second trigger.\', [\'Desk assignment is the shared condition.\', \'It connects enrolled interns to entry codes.\']),\\n      ],\\n      medium: [\\n        question(\'direct-linking-m1\', \'Every registered instrument is calibrated. Every calibrated instrument is tagged. Instrument Q is tagged. What follows about registration?\', [\'Q is registered.\', \'Q is not registered.\', \'Registration is unknown.\'], 2, \'Being at the end of a chain does not establish its beginning.\', [\'Registration guarantees tagging through calibration.\', \'Tagging may also occur without registration.\']),\\n        question(\'direct-linking-m2\', \'Every compact sensor is portable. Every restricted file is unsigned. Which new rule follows?\', [\'Archived file → not restricted.\', \'Archived file → restricted.\', \'Not archived → restricted.\'], 0, \'Contrapose restricted → not signed to expose a signed trigger.\', [\'Restricted → not signed has the equivalent form signed → not restricted.\', \'Archived → signed now links to signed → not restricted, so archived → not restricted.\']),\\n      ],\\n      advanced: [\\n        question(\'direct-linking-a1\', \'Every vessel certified for coastal service is entered in Register C. Insurance can be shared by different groups.\',\\n      \'A shared trigger produces two results, not a new relationship between those results. They do not establish tagged → boxed for all items.\',\\n      \'An apparent mismatch may be real or may be resolved by a valid contrapositive. Check exact scope and subjects before declaring no link. The preceding linking lesson showed how contraposition exposes hidden connections. Use that procedure before deciding that a shared word supplies no valid path.\',\\n    ],\\n    method: [\'Write the direction of each rule.\', \'Compare complete predicates, including qualifiers and subjects.\', \'Look for an actual result-to-trigger connection, including a taught equivalent form.\', \'If none exists, preserve separate rules and draw only conclusions supported by any case fact.\'],\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every approved device is registered.\', \'Every inspected device is registered.\', \'Device J is approved.\'], reasoning: [\'Approval gives registration for J.\', \'Registration is also a result of inspection, not proof of inspection.\'], conclusion: \'J is registered; inspection remains unknown.\' },\\n    guidedExample: question(\'no-link-guide\', \'Every red folder is indexed. Every blue folder is indexed. Which follows?\', [\'Every red folder is blue.\', \'Every indexed folder is red or blue.\', \'Neither cross-group claim follows.\'], 2, \'A shared result does not connect the starting groups.\', [\'Both colors imply indexing.\', \'The rules allow indexed folders of other colors and do not identify one color with the other.\']),\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Turning a common result into a bridge between its possible sources.\', repair: \'Draw the arrows. Two arrowheads meeting at a result do not provide a forward path from one source to the other.\' },\\n    drills: {\\n      beginner: [\\n        question(\'no-link-b1\', \'Every bronze medal is numbered. Every silver medal is numbered. Does bronze imply silver?\', [\'Yes.\', \'No; the shared result supplies no such link.\', \'Yes, if no medal fact is supplied.\'], 1, \'Both arrows point toward numbered.\', [\'Numbering can result from either medal category.\', \'It does not establish a connection between those categories.\']),\\n        question(\'no-link-b2\', \'Every registered boat is painted. Every laminated poster is mounted. Poster D is approved. What follows?\', [\'D is laminated.\', \'D is mounted.\', \'D is not laminated.\'], 1, \'Use D\\\\\'s actual sufficient condition.\', [\'Approval guarantees mounting.\', \'The lamination rule has no established trigger for D.\']),\\n      ],\\n      medium: [\\n        question(\'no-link-m1\', \'Every battery tested at Site A is labelled. Every battery tested at Site B is shipped. Battery J was tested at A. Every connected terminal is logged. Terminal R is not active. Which follows?\', [\'R is not logged.\', \'R is logged.\', \'Logging remains unknown.\'], 2, \'The absence of the first trigger does not settle later results.\', [\'The chain guarantees logging for active terminals.\', \'Inactive terminals may be connected and logged, or neither, consistently with both rules.\']),\\n      ],\\n    },\\n  },\\n];\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 9.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4f2742","wall_time_seconds":8.9268786,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3925,"output":"  {\\r\\n    id: \'contraposition\', title: \'Follow a Missing Requirement Backward\', area: \'inference\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'negation\', \'sufficient-necessary\'],\\r\\n    goal: \'Form an equivalent rule by reversing the conditions and negating both.\',\\r\\n    teaching: [\\r\\n      \'Every verified invoice is recorded. Verified → recorded and not recorded → not verified make the same guarantee.\',\\r\\n      \'To form a contrapositive, reverse the two complete conditions and negate both. If a sensor is active, it is not silent becomes if it is silent, it is not active. Keep the same device and time throughout. Which is equivalent?\', [\'Every timekeeping clock is restored.\', \'Every clock not keeping time is not restored.\', \'Every unrestored clock fails to keep time.\'], 1, \'Begin with absence of the required result.\', [\'Keeping time is required for restoration under this rule.\', \'A clock lacking it cannot be restored.\']),\\r\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Reversing the arrow but leaving both conditions unchanged.\', repair: \'Reverse and negate both. Which is equivalent?\', [\'Overheated → not refrigerated.\', \'Not refrigerated → overheated.\', \'Not overheated → refrigerated.\'], 0, \'The negation of not overheated is overheated.\', [\'Swap the conditions and deny each.\', \'The double negative on overheated cancels.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'contraposition-m2\', \'If a ticket is not cancelled, it is logged. Which is equivalent?\', [\'Not logged → cancelled.\', \'Logged → not cancelled.\', \'Cancelled → not logged.\'], 0, \'Negate the original negative trigger after swapping.\', [\'No logging excludes not cancelled.\', \'Denying not cancelled yields cancelled.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'contraposition-m3\', \'If a signal is not stable, it is not accepted. Which is equivalent?\', [\'Accepted → stable.\', \'Stable → accepted.\', \'Not accepted → not stable.\'], 0, \'Both negatives cancel in the contrapositive.\', [\'The old result\\\\\'s denial is accepted.\', \'That requires denial of not stable, namely stable.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      advanced: [\\r\\n        question(\'contraposition-a1\', \'Every driver certified for Route 8 is listed on its roster. ## assistant

Updated six foundation units: sufficient/necessary, contraposition, Groups 1–2, mixed translation, and direct linking.

Added Joshua’s What Test, Personalized If/Then, six-step translation scaffold, guided prompts requiring those steps, and visible chain alignment.

Preserved IDs, order, item counts, options, and answer keys. Parent should regenerate curriculum JSON.

Source limits: archived GDOCs verify the meaning checks; original six-step PDFs were independently verified. Recall and Rephrase is named in archived homework, but full original instructions were unavailable, so the short practice is explicitly described as an adaptation. Avoided contradictory mechanical shortcuts and logical errors in older PDFs.

Know what each statement does\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:32:| **Fact** | Describes the situation you actually have | Lena is a pilot. |\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:37:### Look for certainty, not just a keyword\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:43:## 2. Chaining: rule + rule produces a new rule\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:54:### Step 1: Diagram each rule\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:61:### Step 2: Find the overlapping idea\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:70:### Step 3: Connect the loose ends\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:78:### A repeated word is not always a bridge\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:98:### Sometimes the bridge is hidden in a contrapositive\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:115:### The answer may test any link you had to build\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:125:## 3. Triggering: fact + rule produces a new fact\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:129:### The fact confirms the trigger\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:133:Fact: Lena is a pilot.\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:140:### The fact denies the required result\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:144:Fact: Lena is not trained.\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:156:### The two facts that do not activate the rule\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:161:Fact: Lena is trained.\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:167:Fact: Lena is not a pilot.\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:174:## 4. Know the four possible moves\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:185:### Use the contrapositive when the move is hidden\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:189:### Diagram when the notation will do work\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:200:### Test a conditional answer by its trigger\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:210:## 5. Use the same process every time\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:218:## Practice\\nstudents/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md:227:## Notes for me (not posted)\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:12:current_student_title: Facts, Rules, and What Follows\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:15:**Current student-facing lesson:** [Facts, Rules, and What Follows](../../../brainstorms/2026-09-30-yahunatan-post-session-recap/conditional-reasoning-v10.html). The earlier versions below are preserved history. The marked V10 block is the current export source.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:17:# Conditional Must Be True: Keep the Whole Statement\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:21:## Combine what the statements guarantee\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:33:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:39:## Put the requirement on the right\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:67:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:73:## Keep should in the statement\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:83:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:89:## Reverse and negate to get the contrapositive\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:107:### Test every possible combination\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:118:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:124:## A statement about a whole group can be a rule\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:142:The middle idea matches exactly. The group rule gets you to unwilling to compromise. The contrapositive takes you from unwilling to compromise to should not undergo mediation.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:146:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:152:## Do not invent a choice between two activities\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:162:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:166:**Worked answer:** No. The person could appropriately pursue neither activity. That violates neither requirement. The premises do not say that mediation and litigation are exhaustive choices, or that rejecting one requires the other.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:168:## Use the same process on a new question\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:174:5. Connect rules only when the ending of one matches the beginning of the next. Apply a fact only when it supplies a valid starting point.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:179:### Put the moves together\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:189:3. Every priority application needs supervisor approval. Approval is required only if the application is incomplete. What follows for priority applications?\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:191:**Worked answer:** Priority → needs approval → incomplete. Every priority application is incomplete under these hypothetical rules. No particular priority application is asserted to exist.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:197:## Before the next session\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:201:## 2026-09-30 concept extension: Choose the move the information supports\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:205:## Apply a fact to a rule\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:207:Suppose every application submitted after the deadline requires special approval. Maya submitted her application after the deadline. Maya\'s application therefore requires special approval.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:211:Late application → requires special approval.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:213:Maya\'s application is late → Maya\'s application requires special approval.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:215:The fact must match the complete condition. If the rule concerns applications submitted after the deadline, knowing that an application was started after the deadline would not be enough. Started and submitted are different claims.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:217:This is the same issue as keeping should in the mediation statement. Actually attending mediation does not establish that someone should attend. A fact cannot activate a rule by matching only part of its wording.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:219:### Check your reasoning\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:221:Every application submitted after the deadline requires special approval. Omar\'s application requires special approval. Must Omar have submitted it after the deadline?\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:223:**Worked answer:** No. Special approval is the result, not the starting condition. Other applications might also require it. An on-time application that needs approval for another reason fits the rule and defeats the proposed inference.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:225:## Find the two starting points a rule gives you\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:227:Use the same rule: late application → requires special approval.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:231:- The application is late. It requires special approval.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:232:- The application does not require special approval. It is not late.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-ze…24340 tokens truncated…not late.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2571:**Contrapositive chain:** Does not need signature → Does not require approval → Was not late.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2573:**Therefore:** This application does not require special approval and was not late.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2575:### A contrapositive can uncover the match\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2591:**New conditional:** Anyone without a badge requires supervision.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2593:Again, that is a general rule. It does not prove that someone lacks a badge. If a separate fact says Niko lacks a badge, then we can apply the chain and conclude that Niko requires supervision.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2597:If the middle condition already matches, link the rules directly. You only need to restate a rule when doing so reveals a connection you could not use as written.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2599:## 08 | Translate the complete claim\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2601:### “Only if” introduces the required condition\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2603:“A visitor may enter only if the visitor has a badge” means permission to enter requires a badge. Write **May enter → Has a badge**. Put the requirement after the arrow. A badge alone does not prove permission to enter.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2607:### Keep requirements separate from completed actions\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2609:“Requires special approval” and “has received special approval” are not the same condition. A requirement can remain unfulfilled. A rule ending in the first phrase cannot be linked to one starting in the second unless another statement connects them. Shared words are not enough; match the complete claim, including qualifiers and negation.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2611:**Try it:** Every late application requires special approval. Every application that has received special approval has a supervisor\'s signature. Does a late application necessarily have a supervisor\'s signature? Explain the missing connection.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2613:**Worked answer:** No. A late application requires approval, but it may not have received it. The second rule applies to applications that have received approval. Nothing says the late application\'s requirement has been fulfilled.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2619:### Rules can connect different kinds of claims\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2635:### Negate the whole requirement\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2637:A condition can contain more than one part. Suppose **a document may be released only if it has been reviewed AND authorized**. Permission requires both parts. If either part is missing, the combined requirement is not satisfied.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2659:## 09 | Choose the move and test the claim\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2661:Ask what you have: a fact that can activate a rule, or two rules that can connect? The matching condition determines the move.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2665:| A fact meeting a rule\'s starting condition | Apply the rule forward | Does the whole fact match the whole condition? |\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2667:| Two rules with a matching middle condition | Link the rules | Does the first rule end with the exact condition the second starts with? |\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2670:To disprove a proposed inference, keep every given statement true and make the proposed conclusion false. For example, an on-time application that requires approval fits **Submitted late → Requires approval**, but disproves **Requires approval → Submitted late**.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2672:## 10 | Mix the moves\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2676:**Practice 1:** Every certified instructor has a license. Every person who is not a certified instructor requires supervision. Quinn has no license. What follows? Show the connection between the rules.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2678:**Worked answer:** Quinn is not a certified instructor and requires supervision. Contrapose the first rule: no license → not a certified instructor. That result meets the second rule\'s starting condition. The chain is no license → not certified → requires supervision. Quinn\'s fact activates it.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2682:**Worked answer:** Sasha may not enter. Permission requires at least one of the two alternatives, and Sasha has neither. An invitation alone would not prove permission: the rule says what permission requires, not that meeting this requirement is sufficient.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2684:**Practice 3:** Every flagged expense requires manager approval. Every expense that has received manager approval may be reimbursed. This expense is flagged. Must this expense be eligible for reimbursement?\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2686:**Worked answer:** No. This expense requires approval, but nothing establishes that it has received approval. It could still be waiting for the manager. The second rule\'s starting condition is not established, so the two phrases cannot serve as a matching middle condition.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2692:## 11 | Take this into your assigned work\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2694:Do the **conditional-diagramming drills first**, then move to the **LSAT questions**. In the drills, write the complete conditions before shortening them. Check your arrow and its contrapositive. When two rules appear, look for an exact match before combining them.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2696:In the LSAT questions, identify the rule or chain that proves each answer. Check that the answer preserves the complete conditions. **Requires approval** does not become **has received approval**. If you cannot prove an answer, try a counterexample: can the given statements be true while the answer is false?\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2698:<!-- END CURRENT STUDENT LESSON V10 -->\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2700:## Notes for me (not posted)\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2714:- **Updated preview:** [Concept-enriched recap](../../../brainstorms/2026-09-30-yahunatan-post-session-recap/conditional-reasoning-v2.html). Draft only; publication awaits approval.\\r\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md:2716:- **2026-09-30 concept-first replacement:** V3 supersedes the earlier question-centered drafts for review and eventual publication. Only the marked V3 lesson is exported. Original drafts remain preserved. [Current concept-first preview](../../../brainstorms/2026-09-30-yahunatan-post-session-recap/conditional-reasoning-v3.html).
