Compare with the original wording and explain any correction before trying another sentence.',\n+      'Recognize related trigger phrasing: whenever, wherever, anytime, in the event that, and the moment that can introduce the triggering event. Each, everyone who, and everything that can specify a whole group. In the event that a sensor restarts, a new log begins means sensor restarts → new log begins. Preserve the complete event and group restrictions.',\n+      'Provided that needs the whole promise: The archive will open provided that power is restored states restored power → archive opens. Guarantees and ensures can also connect a sufficient idea to its result. Leads to, produces, and results in express that direction only when the sentence makes an exceptionless claim; a usual tendency or mere causal possibility is weaker.',\n@@\n       'Use a Personalized If/Then to check your translation. Imagine the display has activated. This rule forces you to know that its battery is charged. Imagine only a charged battery instead: activation is not forced. Keep the original scope and do not fill in an unstated guarantee.',\n+      'Recognize required, prerequisite, necessary, essential, needs, and depends on as requirement phrasing when that is their meaning. A seal is essential for pressure retention gives pressure retained → seal present. Pressure retention depends on a seal gives the same direction. Ask what is required even when the requirement appears first.',\n+      'An expression can name the result without belonging to one fixed word group. Activation implies a recorded event means activation → recorded event, as does Activation means a recorded event under an explicit catalogue convention. Then and will can introduce the result of an if clause. Signals may describe evidence rather than certainty. Identify the guarantee in the full sentence instead of assigning direction from one word alone.',\n@@\n       'Preserve negatives already in the sentence. The gate does not open without a clearance means no clearance → no opening, equivalent to opening → clearance. Until also sets a time boundary: it does not promise that the withheld action happens immediately after the boundary. Without can merely describe circumstances, so first verify what the sentence actually guarantees.',\n+      'Except needs the full claim too. Every crate is retained except damaged crates guarantees not damaged → retained. It does not, by that wording alone, prove what happens to a damaged crate. If the sentence explicitly says damaged crates are discarded, that supplies a separate rule. Keep permission, prohibition, and actual events distinct when an exception concerns who may act.',\n+      'Otherwise or if not can introduce the other case without making a biconditional. If a sensor resets, its log clears; otherwise its log remains unchanged states both cases and supports a two-way relationship between resetting and clearing. Its log clears, otherwise an alert appears supplies only not clear → alert. Translate each stated case rather than assuming both directions.',\n@@\n       'Never and cannot need context. A descriptive rule that the device never runs uncooled excludes that combination. An instruction that a visitor cannot enter means entry is forbidden; it does not physically prevent an unauthorized entry. Keep the claim\\'s modal meaning rather than changing permission into behavior.',\n+      'Not can negate a result inside a universal rule: Paper forms are not accepted means paper form → not accepted. The word not alone does not create a universal rule. This form was not accepted is a case fact, and not all paper forms are accepted says at least one is unaccepted. audiovisual not reviewed. |\\n| 54 | [Adap…1800 tokens truncated…arates the rule from an individual\'s membership fact. | Introduces notation as a representation of already-understood reasoning, not a substitute for comprehension. |\\n| 11: sets versus conditions | Distinguishes categorical membership from events while treating the shared inference form as useful for LSAT work. | Supports transfer between different surface wording. Do not erase semantic differences merely because notation overlaps. |\\n| 12: negation | Distinguishes contradiction from an ordinary opposite and explains double negation. | Prepares the sign changes in contraposition and later compound negation. |\\n| 13–14: contrapositive and argument #2 | Flip direction and negate both conditions to obtain an equivalent rule. A fact denying the necessary condition excludes the sufficient condition. | Teaches a second valid application, rather than an unsupported reversal. |\\n| 15: legal transfer | An edited legal argument illustrates contraposition and highlights a possible hidden semantic assumption. | Transfer still needs interpretation of the actual claims; formal notation does not settle a missing substantive assumption. |\\n| 17–18: English expressions and indicators | Several English forms can express one relationship. Indicator words can be overinclusive or incomplete guides; context determines whether a guarantee is actually present. Not every occurrence of when creates a conditional. | Prevents keyword-only reasoning before four-group practice. |\\n| 19: Group 1 | Four steps: detect the indicator, parse the concepts, assign symbols and apply the translation rule. Contrapose/retranslate optionally. Grammar and economical notation require attention. | Rehearses a process rather than simple word recognition. |\\n| 21: Group 2 | Uses the same procedure for necessary indicators and contrasts sufficient/necessary relationships with valid applications. | Additional scheduled practice targets the direction-sensitive language. The rationale for exact weighting remains inferred. |\\n| 26–27: Group 3 and Or | Negating either complete idea as sufficient can produce equivalent contrapositive forms; retranslation checks meaning. The Or lesson distinguishes inclusive, exclusive and contextual either meanings. | Compound and ordinary-language scope matter beyond a fixed unless recipe. |\\n| 29 and 31: Group 4 and summary | Negate either idea as necessary, then consolidate the four indicator groups. | Group labels support translation; they do not replace checking the complete statement. |\\n| 34: chaining | Demonstrates symbolic and English chains, including four-node chains, multiple supported conclusions and contrapositives. | Extends two-rule connections to intermediate and end-to-end inferences. |\\n| 37–38: combine and review | A valid-conclusion exercise is followed by an invalid argument, exposing why satisfying the necessary condition or failing the sufficient condition gives no forced conclusion. | Integrates the three formal argument forms with tempting invalid moves. |\\n| 39: FAQ | Presents diagrams as a strategic option. Slow deliberate practice builds intuition; revisit it and diagram on the test when complexity makes the notation useful. | Does not prescribe compulsory diagrams for every question. Source numerical frequency claims are not treated as verified test statistics. |\\n| 43: without indicators | Active/passive guarantees and requirement grammar can represent the same rule. Modifiers define the relevant subject subset. The lesson discourages extending memorized word lists indefinitely. | Returns from indicator recognition to semantic parsing. |\\n| 44 and 46: conjunction/disjunction | An AND antecedent requires the joint condition and cannot generally split; an AND consequent can. An OR consequent does not force a particular branch; an OR antecedent can split into separate rules. | Scope determines which decomposition is valid. Teach the operation with its position, not the connective alone. |\\n| 47: De Morgan | Negate the entire compound, swap its connective, negate each component and cancel double negations; restore the contrapositive relationship. | Depends on whole-claim negation and compound scope. |\\n| 49: domain | Separates scope conditions from the active rule and suggests revisiting this technique in later LR study. | LSAT U must verify membership in the restricted domain before suppressing a condition in notation. A scope simplification is not permission to discard premises. |\\n| 51: biconditionals | Contrasts if and only if, combines two directions, and shows equal truth values and the equivalent negative pair. | Exclusive alternatives connect one claim to the negation of the other; they are not an ordinary same-truth-value biconditional. |\\n\\nThe parent launched the Sufficiency and Necessity Workshop\'s Start control inside its iframe. Four scenario choices lead into constructing nested boxes to represent universal inclusion. Instructions permit moving boxes and resizing them from a lower-right corner. Initial feedback diagnoses an oversized subset box and prompts shrinking it; a statement-building dialog also appears. No boxes were moved and no task was completed, so later corrective behavior, task count and completion feedback remain unverified. This mechanism rehearses set containment through manipulation rather than only writing an arrow. You Try entrances promise an attempt followed by explanation. The Coffee Shoppe response screen was inspected, as described above; explanation/feedback screens were not. Their actual transfer feedback remains a gap in this research.\\n\\n## Practice adds cumulative syntax and reasoning demands\\n\\nGroup 3\'s five prompts cover without, unless and until, including an existing negation and a domain restriction. The first Answer reveal was inspected and showed both equivalent directions. Group 4\'s five prompts cover no, none and never with longer modified concepts. Group 1\'s first worksheet reveal likewise showed diagram and contrapositive. Full feedback for every prompt was not individually revealed.\\n\\nEach chaining worksheet has five prompts. Inspection found reordered rules and contraposition, nonconnecting premises, irrelevant information, and a distinction between being expected to do something and being able to do it. The practice therefore tests parsing and whether a link is licensed, rather than merely connecting repeated letters.\\n\\nDraw Valid Conclusions 1 has five prompts asking the learner to translate, combine supported conclusions and translate back. It includes double negation, concrete facts and implicit requirements.\\n\\nThe compound worksheet has six prompts, correcting the earlier incomplete count. The parent inspected all six Answer reveals. Their demands include an only-if rule with a joint outcome; contradiction versus an ordinary opposite; a modified noun represented as either an implicit conjunction or one complete concept; a temporal restriction kept in the joint antecedent; not-only/but conjunction; an age range optionally expressed with disjunction; and a joint trigger alongside a distracting possibility claim that supplies no guarantee. Feedback provides written reasoning for manual comparison; autograding was not observed. The training therefore requires interpretation and scope beyond keyword lists. Its embedded video runtime is 15:16, compared with a 30-minute schedule estimate; the video was not watched.\\n\\nPre-hydration DOM snapshots can expose hidden answer text and lose negation styling. This research does not diagnose an incorrect answer from plain DOM alone. It relies on inspected semantics and the verified reveal interactions, while preserving the remaining feedback limits.\\n\\n## Preserve the useful distinctions and adjudicate source shortcuts\\n\\nThe inspected Other Conjunction Indicators page treats but, even if and even though as AND. Do not copy that as a universal semantic rewrite. Even if does not establish that its antecedent is true; even though usually presents that clause as a fact. Any LSAT U treatment should distinguish the context-specific logical relationship rather than make a blanket connective substitution.\\n\\nThe domain simplification also needs an explicit boundary: the rule is applicable only after its domain condition is established. Likewise, quantified all-to-most-to-some shorthand requires the relevant existence assumption, addressed in [the quantity analysis](research-7sage-quantifiers.md). These are original analytical cautions, not claims that a title alone proves the source wrong.\\n\\nThe analysis separates these dimensions:\\n\\n- **Skill:** the operation the student learns, based on inspected teaching rather than title alone.\\n- **Prerequisites:** explicitly stated prerequisites separately from inferred dependencies.\\n- **Instruction:** what reasoning is explained, the distinction it makes and any error it rules out.\\n- **Training mechanism:** demonstration, worksheet, classification, derivation, guided attempt or independent check.\\n- **Practice and feedback:** visible task count, what the answer reveal explains, and whether both diagram and contrapositive appear.\\n- **Transition:** how the resource prepares for the next resource; label inference when the platform does not state it.\\n- **Evidence:** text read, answer reveal inspected, video-only/unread audiovisual, or title/order metadata only.\\n\\nNo source example sentences, full captions, transcripts or item banks are copied here. Opening a lesson is not completing it. A title and duration do not establish the depth of teaching. The overview is the only video with a complete caption read in this report; other video content remains unreviewed. Worksheet reveals, written articles and entrance screens have the separate limits recorded above. Source summaries are concise original paraphrases; repeated discussion of a resource remains below 200 attributed words per source, including the overview summary and ledger metadata.\\n\\n## How to assess relative emphasis\\n\\nThe observed order emphasizes semantic foundations before notation, then positive indicator translation, negative indicator translation, chaining and combined arguments, followed by compound scope and biconditionals. Real-question attempts and adaptive practice punctuate the sequence. Known worksheet time estimates establish a substantial cumulative translation block and a separate chaining block; the other durations are unavailable in this report. A resource can cover several operations, so resource counts are not independent skill counts. Teaching minutes, practice estimates and verified task counts must remain separate.\\n\\nResource order can suggest the platform\'s instructional progression, but it does not prove that every learner must master one before accessing another. State whether sequencing is enforced or simply presented only when inspected.\\n\\n## Original LSAT U coverage proposal from existing local evidence\\n\\nThese are recommendations, not competitor findings or final curriculum decisions. Beginner / Medium / Advanced are broad teaching stages. Each should contain both a complete pathway assignment and independently assignable specific-skill subsections.\\n\\n| Stage | Teach and assess | Existing material to reuse | Content or packaging needed |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| Beginner | Rule versus fact; sufficient/necessary; what is forced versus left possible; Group 1/2 translation; simple negation and contraposition; Group 3/4; all four ways a fact can relate to a rule | Refresher cheat sheet/sorting/diagrams; selected General items; MBT lecture opening and fact-triggering applications | Short coherent notes, isolated valid/invalid application practice, specific Group 3 assignment, fresh diagnostic and clear untimed mastery criteria |\\n| Medium | Connect compatible rules; expose links through contraposition; preserve negative links; distinguish shared result from a bridge; find a missing premise; combine a chain with a fact; AND/OR scope | Hub Linking/Missing Link/MBT banks; SA chain exercises; MBT lecture | Separate operations currently mixed together, worked examples with fading scaffolds, no-link contrast items and targeted fresh transfer pools |\\n| Advanced | Nested/compound negation; alternative branches; biconditionals; multiple chains and irrelevant rules; precision under natural-language complexity; transfer across MBT/MSS, SA, principle, parallel and flaw | Harder General items, compound hub questions, SA chapters, guided real-question pools | Operation-level audit, original gaps filled after review, unseen transfer tasks and speed practice after accuracy |\\n| Quantifiers, next phase | Meaning and scope of all/none/some/most; existence assumptions; direction and negation; valid/invalid quantified combinations; mixed quantified/conditional inferences | Partial legacy Some/Most material and MBT scope examples | Dedicated source-backed original lesson sequence and answer-keyed independent practice; confirm prerequisite and placement relative to the three stages |\\n\\nDo not use a single overall percentage as the only placement evidence. Distinguish translation direction, negation, scope, chaining, triggering and application. Successful repetition of an explained question is review evidence; a fresh structurally related item supplies stronger transfer evidence.\\n\\n## A subsection should be a complete learning unit\\n\\nProposed independently assignable subsection structure: a short explanation of one skill, one worked original example, a guided attempt, fresh independent practice, immediate move-specific feedback, and an exit check. The teacher should be able to assign the whole stage or only the failed skill. The student should see why that subsection is next and what would count as readiness to progress.\\n\\nExisting whole-resource assignments, browser-local refresher progress, saved hub practice and General indicator ledgers are different mechanisms. A unified curriculum needs to connect them; it should not silently claim they already form an adaptive mastery pathway.\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

Recommend seven chapters, preserving all 30 content IDs and practice banks:

| Chapter | Existing units | Rich teaching and interaction |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Understand the guarantee | `orientation`, `rules-facts`, `sufficient-necessary`, `truth-validity` | Explain a conditional in plain English; separate trigger/result; contrast rule/fact; predict what follows before revealing why. |
| 2. Use a fact with a rule | `negation`, `contraposition`, `triggering` | Keep one rule fixed while switching among four fact cases. Compare valid forward application, contrapositive, and two unknown cases. |
| 3. Translate the actual meaning | Groups 1–4, `mixed-translation`, `implicit-conditionals` | Four distinct sublessons using What Test, Personalized If/Then, six-step scaffold, indicator-target highlights, and English rephrasing. Add cumulative practice after Groups 1–2 and again after Groups 3–4. |
| 4. Connect rules | `direct-linking`, `no-link` | Show separate rules, expose exact overlap, reveal the new rule, then add a case fact. Contrast shared trigger/result, hidden contraposition, and requirement versus completion. |
| 5. Handle complex conditions | `and-or`, `de-morgan`, `domain`, `biconditionals`, `whole-rule-negation` | Branch diagrams, compound truth cases, negate-one-part comparisons, domain boundaries, two directions versus one rule, and a counterexample to the whole rule. |
| 6. Apply the reasoning | `mbt`, `sufficient-assumption`, `necessary-assumption`, `flaw`, `parallel`, `principle` | Task-specific demonstrations followed by targeted existing Activities and real-question drills. Distinguish deriving a fact, filling a sufficient bridge, testing a necessary assumption, and diagnosing an invalid move. |
| 7. Extend to quantities | Four quantifier units | Meaning and witness examples, precise negation, valid combinations, and invalid chains. Comes after conditionals; independently assignable. |

The opening **Find the Guarantee** should become a real explanatory note, not an introductory question card. Suggested sections: **What a conditional says → What the trigger guarantees → A rule does not prove its trigger occurred → What remains possible → Read the same rule in different wording → Try it and explain why**. Subsequent sublessons introduce the remaining nuances rather than forcing everything into the opening.

The actual Intro model is visible in [IntroLessonPage.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/modules/lessons/notes/IntroLessonPage.tsx:348): its first note introduces the structural triad, then uses an anatomy scanner. “So, An Argument” explains premises/conclusions before its support simulator; “Premises” separates fact meaning from evidence role before its inline check. Reuse that explanation-before-interaction pattern.

Reuse the editorial note shell, named TOC sections, worked contrasts, and integrated note-to-lab navigation from [IntroNotesShell.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/modules/lessons/notes/IntroNotesShell.tsx:1) and [introToLrPath.ts](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/content/introToLrPath.ts:32). Extract the shell presentation rather than importing Intro’s hard-coded path/completion behavior or conclusion-specific launch copy.

Main gaps: current teaching strings need editorial subdivision into explanatory sections; existing guided questions are not substitutes for purpose-built demonstrations; cumulative checkpoints need packaging; older Activity banks need precise mapping before claiming group-specific drill coverage. Keep teacher assignment separate from the pedagogical sequence. No edits made.

A current pass.', rule: 'Entry → current pass.', why: 'The required idea appears before required. Having a pass alone does not guarantee entry.' },\n+  'group-3': { sentence: 'The lamp does not light unless the battery is charged.', target: 'The two complete ideas are lamp does not light and battery is charged.', rule: 'Battery not charged → lamp does not light. Equivalently: lamp lights → battery charged.', why: 'Keep the existing negative. Negating does not light gives lights when you form the equivalent direction.' },\n+  'group-4': { sentence: 'No approved sample is unlabelled.', target: 'The excluded pair is approved sample and unlabelled sample.', rule: 'Approved sample → not unlabelled, or labelled.', why: 'Negating unlabelled cancels its existing negative. The rules establish calibrated → listed.</p>\n+    <div className=\"cn-controls\"><button type=\"button\" aria-pressed={fact} onClick={() => setFact(!fact)}>{fact ? 'Remove the case fact' : 'Add: Meter D is calibrated'}</button></div>\n+    <div className=\"cn-result\" aria-live=\"polite\"><p><strong>{fact ? 'Meter D is registered and listed.' : 'Every calibrated meter is listed.'}</strong></p><p>{fact ? 'The separate fact activates the chain for D. Both intermediate and final results follow.' : 'This is a new rule. This exploration does not grade your written reasoning.</p>\n+  </div>;\n+}\n+\n+export function LessonInteraction({ unit }: { unit: ConditionalUnit }) {\n+  if (translations[unit.id]) return state.progress!.lessons[unit.id] = \'2026-10-01T12:00:00.000Z\';\\r\\n  state.progress!.attempts.push(check(unit.guidedExample.id, unit.id));\\r\\n  return unit;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalLessonNote.test.tsx\n+import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen } from '@testing-library/react';\n+import { MemoryRouter } from 'react-router-dom';\n+import { afterEach, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';\n+import { conditionalUnits } from '../content';\n+import { ConditionalLessonNote } from './ConditionalLessonNote';\n+vi.mock('@/components/ui/dynamic-island-toc', () => ({ DynamicIslandTOC: () => null }));\n+afterEach(cleanup);\n+\n+function mount(id: string) {\n+  const unit = conditionalUnits.find(item => item.id === id)!;\n+  const complete = vi.fn();\n+  render(<MemoryRouter><ConditionalLessonNote unit={unit} onComplete={complete} reviewed={false} continueHref=\"/guided\" /></MemoryRouter>);\n+  return { unit, complete };\n+}\n+\n+it('explains a conditional before the case explorer and preserves every teaching paragraph', () => {\n+  const { unit, complete } = mount('orientation');\n+  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'A promise about qualifying cases' })).toBeTruthy();\n+  unit.teaching.forEach(paragraph => expect(screen.getByText(paragraph)).toBeTruthy());\n+  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Its tracking record was updated.' }));\n+  expect(screen.getByText('Whether the parcel was scanned remains unknown.')).toBeTruthy();\n+  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Its tracking record was not updated.' }));\n+  expect(screen.getByText('The parcel was not scanned.')).toBeTruthy();\n+  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n+  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Mark lesson as read' }));\n+  expect(complete).toHaveBeenCalledOnce();\n+});\n+\n+it('checks the Group 2 target before revealing direction without a difficulty selector', () => {\n+  mount('group-2');\n+  expect(screen.queryByText('Entry → current pass.')).toBeNull();\n+  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Find the target' }));\n+  expect(screen.getByText('What is required? A current pass.')).toBeTruthy();\n+  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Show the translation' }));\n+  expect(screen.getByText('Entry → current pass.')).toBeTruthy();\n+  expect(screen.queryByRole('combobox')).toBeNull();\n+});\n+\n+it('distinguishes a chain rule from the new facts established by adding a case', () => {\n+  mount('direct-linking');\n+  expect(screen.getByText('Every calibrated meter is listed.')).toBeTruthy();\n+  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add: Meter D is calibrated' }));\n+  expect(screen.getByText('Meter D is registered and listed.')).toBeTruthy();\n+});\n*** End Patch"));
